# Triage report: numpy/numpy

2251 open items (1917 issues, about 1802 distinct problems). Proposals only: nothing here changes GitHub.

## How this report is made

1. **Crawl repo.** Every issue and PR of the repository, open and closed, is copied with its comments into a local database, together with the links GitHub itself keeps (which PR closes which issue, which issue a maintainer marked as a duplicate). Code is never read.
2. **Read and trim.** Each item becomes one vector from its title, its description and a short digest of its comments, made by a small local language-embedding model. Items about the same thing end up close to each other.
3. **Compare and group.** Pairs that are very close are proposed as duplicates, as an old bug coming back, or as an issue and the PR that fixed it without saying so. GitHub's own links are used as they are.
4. **Suggest label.** A missing label comes from the title ("BUG:", "f2py") or from the labels of the most similar labeled items.
5. **Score and check.** Each proposal gets the algorithm's score; person and LLM recheck afterwards.

## Tech used

- **Open issues already marked as duplicates**: GitHub GraphQL `MarkedAsDuplicateEvent`: a maintainer's own action.
- **Open issues whose closing PR is merged**: GitHub GraphQL `closingIssuesReferences` and "fixes #N" in the PR's description, PR merged (high confidence means the link is most likely true, neither the issue nor the PR is done).
- **Likely duplicates of an older open issue**: bge-small-en embeddings (title + description, averaged with an extractive MMR digest of the comments); high similarity between two open issues (confidence = similarity).
- **Open issues very similar to an older closed one (came back, or a duplicate of something settled?)**: bge-small-en embeddings (title + description, averaged with an extractive MMR digest of the comments); high similarity to an older closed issue (confidence = similarity).
- **Open issues that a later merged PR seems to fix**: bge-small-en embeddings (title + description, averaged with an extractive MMR digest of the comments); the issue and a later merged PR are each other's nearest neighbor, high similarity, no closing link (confidence = similarity).
- **Open PRs after the same thing**: GitHub closing links to the same issue, or bge-small-en embeddings (title + description, averaged with an extractive MMR digest of the comments) with high similarity between open PRs of different authors (confidence = similarity).
- **Labels to add**: title rules learned from labeled items (prefix such as BUG:/ENH:, a component's name; confidence = the rule's hit rate); type: kNN over the nearest labeled items (confidence = weighted share carrying the label); component: votes of the nearest labeled items plus linked items (confidence = share of the votes).

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31868">#31868</a> BUG: `numpy.ma.MaskedArray` arithmetic with Python scalars differs from `ndarray`</td><td>duplicate of the older open #27258 (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe MaskedArray arithmetic with Python scalars incorrectly promoting dtypes (int8→int64, float32→float64) unlike ndarray, due to getdata() converting scalars to strong 0-d arrays before ufuncs.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/32372">#32372</a> BUG: `numpy.linalg.norm` overflows for intermediate values due to naive sum-of-squares implementation</td><td>duplicate of the older open #8775 (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues report the identical overflow bug in numpy.linalg.norm due to naive sum-of-squares and propose the same max-scaling fix; A&#x27;s comments explicitly note it duplicates #8775.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/33203">#33203</a> BUG: `np.bincount` with float weights returns integers for empty input</td><td>duplicate of the older open #6854 (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues concern dtype handling in np.bincount with weights, but A is a bug in empty-input corner case returning int instead of float, while B is a feature request about general dtype promotion behavior.</td></tr>
</table></details>

<details><summary><h2 style="display:inline;border:none;padding:0;margin:0">Open issues very similar to an older closed one (came back, or a duplicate of something settled?) (62)</h2></summary>

<tr><th>Item</th><th>Proposal (confidence)</th><th>Check</th></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/32272">#32272</a> DOC: `.npz` zip container is unspecified</td><td>like #32270, closed as not planned (0.97)</td><td><b>same</b>, Issue A is a re-filed, more concise version of Issue B after the maintainer closed B and asked the author to open a new, briefer issue.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/9775">#9775</a> einsum_path &#x27;optimal&#x27; not optimal in at least one case</td><td>like #9769, closed as completed (0.96)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve einsum_path &#x27;optimal&#x27; not finding the best contraction path, but A is due to memory constraints limiting search space while B is a specific algorithmic bug.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/19308">#19308</a> Protential memory leak in function PyInit__multiarray_tests (a static analysis report)</td><td>like #19307, closed as completed (0.96)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both are static analysis reports of potential memory leaks in module initialization functions where a module reference is not decremented on error paths, but they occur in different modules (_multiarray_tests vs multiarray_umath).</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/25677">#25677</a> BUG: Masked array default fill value can overflow</td><td>like #17697, closed as completed (0.95)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve the default integer fill value (999999) in numpy.ma, but Issue A is about overflow when the fill value exceeds the array dtype&#x27;s range (e.g., int8, float16), while Issue B is about the fill value&#x27;s internal dtype being int32 causing overflow when setting larger values on int64 arrays.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/27250">#27250</a> BUG: sort(kind=&#x27;stable&#x27;) for structured arrays is NOT stable</td><td>like #26387, closed as completed (0.95)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues report the same misunderstanding: users expect `order=&#x27;field&#x27;` to sort only by that field, but it actually sorts by that field first then other fields.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/28920">#28920</a> BUG: `array_equal(x, y, equal_nan=True)` raises for structured arrays</td><td>like #8192, closed as completed (0.95)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues stem from the same root cause: `isnan` ufunc doesn&#x27;t support structured dtypes, breaking NaN-aware equality checks in `array_equal(equal_nan=True)` and `assert_array_equal`.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31926">#31926</a> BUG: Signed integer overflow in datetime.c - comprehensive fix needed for all arithmetic operations</td><td>like #31686, closed as completed (0.95)</td><td><b>related</b>, Issue A is a follow-up to Issue B, identifying the same overflow pattern exists throughout datetime.c beyond the specific days_to_yearsdays() fix in #31688.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/9331">#9331</a> assert_array_equal does not compare NaNs as numbers when dtype is object</td><td>like #8192, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues report that assert_array_equal fails to treat NaN as equal for non-standard dtypes (object and structured), indicating the same underlying bug.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/17224">#17224</a> Creating object arrays of ragged depth fails</td><td>like #15041, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both concern ragged array handling with object dtype, but A is about explicit dtype=object failing for nested arrays of different shapes, while B is about deprecation warnings for automatic object dtype from ragged lists.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/17305">#17305</a> `concatenate` doesn&#x27;t understand `dict_values`</td><td>like #2612, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>same</b>, Issue A explicitly references Issue B (#2612) as the previous report of the same problem with `np.concatenate` not accepting `dict_values`.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/18147">#18147</a> Issues with np.unique when dtype=&quot;object&quot;</td><td>like #15199, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve np.unique failing on object arrays with incomparable types, but A also reports a separate NaN handling issue.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/21791">#21791</a> BUG: numpy.concatenate casts a list of integers to floats if concatenated with the empty list (it shouldn&#x27;t do this)</td><td>like #8878, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe numpy.concatenate casting integers to floats when concatenated with an empty list due to np.array([]) defaulting to float64.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/24050">#24050</a> BUG: assert_equal does not distinguish scalars from 0-dim arrays</td><td>like #3887, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues concern lenient scalar handling in numpy.testing assert functions, but A focuses on scalar vs 0-dim array type distinction in assert_equal, while B focuses on shape-checking bypass for scalars in assert_array_equal.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/28839">#28839</a> BUG: nanmin/nanmax with object dtype comparing nans</td><td>like #3508, closed as completed (0.94)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe nanmin/nanmax failing to properly ignore NaN values in object dtype arrays due to the lack of a reliable isnan implementation for object dtypes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31210">#31210</a> BUG: `Generator.choice(replace=False, p=...)` silently ignores `shuffle` parameter</td><td>like #26254, closed as not planned (0.94)</td><td><b>related</b>, Issue A reports a bug where `shuffle` is silently ignored when `p` is provided; Issue B questions `shuffle=False` behavior and notes uniform weights cause shuffling (which is actually the Issue A bug manifesting).</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/5041">#5041</a> return NaN in npy_ObjectMax() and npy_ObjectMin() if it&#x27;s an argument</td><td>like #4903, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Issue B fixed Python 2&#x27;s arbitrary NaN comparison; issue A addresses the remaining semantic mismatch with documentation and Python&#x27;s min/max (return first NaN), using the same patch.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/7329">#7329</a> MaskedArray.copy Shares Fill Value</td><td>like #1989, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both involve fill_value copying behavior in masked arrays, but A is a bug in copy() sharing fill_value, while B is a design decision not to copy fill_value during field assignment.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/7330">#7330</a> BUG: `np.median` and `np.average` don&#x27;t work on masked arrays directly</td><td>like #4445, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues concern `np.median` behavior on masked arrays, but A reports it ignores masks entirely while B reports it incorrectly returns the `masked` constant; different symptoms, same function domain.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/9465">#9465</a> BUG: Wrong results from histogram for small weights</td><td>like #6040, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve histogram precision loss from cumulative sum/diff algorithm with weighted data, but A specifically concerns float32 underflow while B (fixed) concerned float64 dynamic range.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/10004">#10004</a> datetime64/timedelta64 do not accept numpy integer types</td><td>like #7462, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve numpy integer types not being accepted by datetime64/timedelta64 constructors, but B was fixed for timedelta64 while A remains open for datetime64.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/12098">#12098</a> assert_array_equal(x,y) fails for some object arrays</td><td>like #8323, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both involve assert_array_equal failing on nested arrays due to ambiguous truth values from element-wise array comparisons, but B was a specific deprecation-warning pass-through bug that was fixed, while A reports the general limitation.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/13011">#13011</a> np.linalg.inv doesn&#x27;t throw error on singular matrix inversion</td><td>like #8769, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues show np.linalg.inv failing to raise an error for singular matrices due to LAPACK&#x27;s LU decomposition with partial pivoting not reliably detecting singularity in floating-point arithmetic.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/13683">#13683</a> np.full(..., dtype=structured-dtype) does not work as expected</td><td>like #6366, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve np.full mishandling non-scalar fill values, but A concerns structured dtypes while B concerns object arrays and dtype inference.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/14828">#14828</a> Improving the error message when adding i8 array to u8 array</td><td>like #7225, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both involve the same ufunc casting error message but address different aspects: A requests clearer error messaging for mixed integer types, while B documents a breaking casting rule change requiring downstream fixes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/15726">#15726</a> Documentation for numpy.fromfunction induces an erroneous interpretation!</td><td>like #7622, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues report the same misleading documentation in numpy.fromfunction that suggests the function is called per coordinate when it&#x27;s actually called once with coordinate arrays.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/17118">#17118</a> zipfile with inner binary file could not be read numpy.fromfile</td><td>like #3207, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the same limitation of np.fromfile requiring a file descriptor and failing with zipfile objects; B was explicitly closed as a duplicate of A.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/19205">#19205</a> array_equal or testing.assert_array_equal does not compare NaNs as numbers when dtype is object</td><td>like #7944, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both involve NaN comparison in object arrays, but A is about array_equal/assert_array_equal failures with equal_nan=True while B was about np.equal behavior (now fixed).</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/19472">#19472</a> numpy.linalg.eigh fails to properly diagonalize some matrices while raising no error</td><td>like #12738, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, same function, linalg.eigh on large matrices; silent wrong result vs convergence error. <b>different</b>, Issue A involves silent incorrect results on a massive 7GB matrix (likely 32-bit BLAS integer overflow), while Issue B raised a convergence error on a 4200x4200 matrix and was fixed by newer OpenBLAS.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/20514">#20514</a> BUG: Rounding floats which are already equal to an integer changes the value</td><td>like #7355, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, both float rounding; the round algorithm vs how the value prints. <b>different</b>, Issue A is a numerical accuracy bug in np.round algorithm changing integer float values, while Issue B is a scalar repr display discrepancy showing extra digits.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/21065">#21065</a> BUG: Checking if dtype is in a set of dtypes fails, but same check with list passes.</td><td>like #16208, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the identical bug where dtype equality works for list membership but fails for set membership due to hash(dtype) != hash(scalar_type) despite dtype == scalar_type.</td></tr>

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/23546">#23546</a> BUG: Error not raised for an unsafe cast when using casting=&quot;safe&quot;</td><td>like #22985, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve casting=&#x27;safe&#x27; allowing precision loss, but A is int64→float64 integer precision loss while B is float64→float32 float precision loss.</td></tr>

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/25548">#25548</a> BUG: masked_array mean/sum not aligned with array</td><td>like #3927, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues concern masked_array.mean() behavior but address different problems: A is about dtype promotion during computation (int16 vs float64 causing overflow), B is about return type consistency (np.float64 vs MaskedArray across dimensions).</td></tr>

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/26401">#26401</a> ENH: Change behavior for `numpy.polynomial.polynomial.Polynomial.fit` coefficients</td><td>like #21782, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues address the confusing behavior of Polynomial.fit() returning coefficients in the scaled domain instead of the original data domain, requiring .convert() for expected results.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/26761">#26761</a> BUG: type cast behaviour change in NumPy 2.0</td><td>like #26596, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both involve stricter bounds checking for Python integers in NumPy 2.0, but A concerns in-place arithmetic operations while B concerns array construction/conversion.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/27498">#27498</a> BUG: `randint` on Windows appears to require int32 not int</td><td>like #17038, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the same core problem: RandomState.randint on Windows uses 32-bit C long, causing ValueError for large integers that exceed int32 bounds.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/27985">#27985</a> BUG: np.arange give wrong result when given big integer</td><td>like #19552, closed as not planned (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, both np.arange giving wrong results; big integers vs decimal steps. <b>different</b>, A involves integer overflow in length calculation for large int64 values exceeding 53-bit float precision, while B involves floating-point representation errors with decimal step sizes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/28104">#28104</a> allclose() fails on structured arrays</td><td>like #4107, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve allclose/isclose failing on non-float dtypes (structured arrays vs strings/integers), but the specific dtypes and error mechanisms differ.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/28371">#28371</a> Issue with numpy and pandas when running the python 3.12.8</td><td>like #18748, closed as completed (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, both ImportError when importing numpy; different causes. <b>different</b>, Different error messages and root causes - A is about importing from numpy source directory, B is about missing C extensions from mixed package managers.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/10496">#10496</a> varying Errors while changing the type of 0 as a step in arange</td><td>like #5775, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues report inconsistent error handling in np.arange when step=0 across different dtypes (floats in A, integers in B), indicating the same underlying bug in arange&#x27;s length calculation.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/11881">#11881</a> numpy.round(i, decimals=d) wrong for np.int64 and d&lt;0</td><td>like #5903, closed as not planned (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Issue B was explicitly closed as a duplicate of issue A, both describing numpy.round incorrectly using float conversion for int64 with negative decimals.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/15601">#15601</a> BUG: ma.corrcoef produces wrong results and takes very long.</td><td>like #3336, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues report the same core bug in ma.corrcoef: inconsistent handling of masked data where means use per-variable masks but covariances use pairwise masks, producing incorrect correlations.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/16499">#16499</a> BUG: numpy.pad pad constant is cast to input array dtype </td><td>like #10118, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, both np.pad casting the constant to the wrong dtype. <b>different</b>, A concerns constant value casting to input dtype during padding, while B concerns dtype inference for empty array inputs.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/16665">#16665</a> Empty arrays should repr valid code to recreate them</td><td>like #8316, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the same problem where empty array repr includes an invalid `shape` keyword argument for the `array()` constructor, making `eval(repr(arr))` fail.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/18700">#18700</a> BUG: `where=` in gufuncs is slightly broken with `out=` and casts</td><td>like #14659, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both involve ufunc/gufunc internals with `where` and `out` parameters, but A is a gufunc regression with casts while B is documented uninitialized-memory behavior in regular ufuncs.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/19146">#19146</a> BUG/API: np.array([0, max_uint64]) has float64 dtype</td><td>like #14883, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the same dtype inference behavior where mixing int64-compatible and uint64-only values results in float64, and issue B is explicitly closed as a duplicate of issue A.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/20684">#20684</a> ENH: numpy.mean() gives inconsistant results with and without &#x27;axis&#x27; argument</td><td>like #9393, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe inconsistent mean/sum results caused by pairwise summation along the fast axis, which is a known design trade-off for higher precision.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/22956">#22956</a> BUG: np.mean(axis=0) return incorrect value for a large size float32 array</td><td>like #5681, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues involve float32 precision loss in mean/sum for large arrays, but B was fixed for the general case while A persists for axis-specific reductions due to different accumulation algorithms.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/23518">#23518</a> DOC: Representing big float32 numbers as string</td><td>like #18608, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the same floating-point string representation behavior where large float16/float32 values print as rounded numbers due to the &#x27;shortest unique representation&#x27; algorithm.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/24976">#24976</a> ENH: Ensure that output of np.clip has the same dtype as the main array</td><td>like #24253, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe np.clip unexpectedly changing output dtype due to type promotion; B is a specific bug instance fixed by NEP 50, while A is a broader enhancement discussion referencing the same fix.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/25606">#25606</a> DOC: See if we should make a note of `__array_wrap__(..., return_scalar=True)` in subclassing docs</td><td>like #5819, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe ndarray subclasses returning wrapped subclass instances (0-d arrays) instead of bare scalars for reduction operations like .sum().</td></tr>

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/29125">#29125</a> CI: Enable GitHub Actions CI for `ppc64le` (Power architecture) support</td><td>like #28438, closed as duplicate (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues address adding ppc64le CI support to NumPy, but Issue A proposes GitHub-hosted runners via IBM&#x27;s actionspz while Issue B proposed a self-hosted OSU VM runner, leading to different maintainer responses and outcomes.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/29336">#29336</a> BUG: numpy.unique ignores &#x27;equal_nan&#x27; parameter while &#x27;axis&#x27; presents</td><td>like #23286, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe numpy.unique ignoring equal_nan parameter when axis is specified; Issue B was closed with a 1D fix but 2D case remained, and Issue A reports the same remaining 2D failure.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31037">#31037</a> BUG: `np.result_type(x, int) != np.result_type(x, 1)`</td><td>like #27325, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both issues describe the same core behavior: np.result_type treats Python scalar types (int, float) as dtypes (defaulting to int64/float64) while scalar values (1, 1.0) follow NEP 50 weak promotion rules.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31485">#31485</a> Mailmap updates for the NumPy 2.5.0 release.</td><td>like #20359, closed as completed (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, Both issues are mailmap updates for different NumPy releases (2.5.0 vs 1.22.0) with different contributor lists.</td></tr>

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/22611">#22611</a> BUG: Increase accuracy of log1p for small inputs</td><td>#26798 (BUG: Rewrite complex log1p to improve precision.) also closes #22609: keep one (0.99)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both PRs address the same issue (gh-22609) to improve complex log1p precision using double-double arithmetic near |z+1|=1, with PR B implementing the approach discussed in PR A.</td></tr>

<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/30281">#30281</a> BUG: Return first NaN occurence in np.unique for complex arrays</td><td>#30496 (ENH: Hash-based np.unique with support for return_index/return_inverse/return_counts) also closes #30113: keep one (0.99)</td><td><b>overlap</b>, Both PRs address issue #30113 (NaN handling in np.unique), but PR B is a comprehensive hash-based rewrite that would subsume PR A&#x27;s targeted bug fix.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31015">#31015</a> BUG: fix norm with ord=-2 crashing on empty matrices</td><td>#31022 (BUG: Handle empty matrices in linalg.norm for ord=-2, -1, -inf) also closes #30916: keep one (0.99)</td><td><b>overlap</b>, Both fix the same empty matrix crash for ord=-2 (issue #30916) but with different implementations; PR B also covers ord=-1 and ord=-inf.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/32790">#32790</a> BUG: support array_like inputs in np.split and related functions</td><td>#33140 (BUG: fix split and array_split for list input) also closes #17463: keep one (0.99)</td><td><b>same</b>, Both PRs fix the same issue (#17463) - making split functions accept array_like inputs - with slightly different implementations but equivalent user-facing results.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/28567">#28567</a> BUG: Fix NEP50 weak-type scalar logic for binary operations on masked arrays</td><td>looks like #31871 (BUG: MaskedArray arithmetic with Python scalars ignores NEP 50 weak-type) by another author (0.93)</td><td><b>overlap</b>, PR A (#28567) is a comprehensive fix for NEP50 weak-type scalar logic in both in-place and non-in-place binary operations on masked arrays, while PR B (#31871) is a narrower fix addressing only non-in-place operations using a different call-site approach; they overlap in fixing non-in-place binary operations.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/28608">#28608</a> ENH: Convert unary from C universal intrinsics to C++ using Highway</td><td>looks like #28718 (ENH: Convert unary_fp from C universal intrinsics to C++ using Highway) by another author (0.93)</td><td><b>related</b>, one series converting unary loops to Highway, merged in order, not competing. <b>independent</b>, PR A converts general unary operations while PR B converts floating-point unary operations specifically, targeting different operation subsets.</td></tr>
<tr><td><a href="https://github.com/numpy/numpy/issues/31871">#31871</a> BUG: MaskedArray arithmetic with Python scalars ignores NEP 50 weak-type</td><td>looks like #33253 (BUG: keep Python scalars weak in MaskedArray comparisons) by another author (0.92)</td><td><b>related</b>, sibling fixes with the same root cause (NEP 50 weak scalars lost in masked arrays). <b>independent</b>, PR A fixes arithmetic operations (_MaskedBinaryOperation.__call__ and power) while PR B fixes comparison operations (MaskedArray._comparison); both address getdata() converting Python scalars to strong types but in separate code paths.</td></tr>
</table></details>
